## Execution model — preserve configured routing

Operator clarification (2026-09-06): Claude Code usually completes planning; Hermes uses Herdr to
assign an issue/plan to Claude Code, OMP, or Cursor-agent using dependencies and quota. The selected
worker owns the remaining Busdriver pipeline through delivery. This records the operator's deployed
workflow; it does not claim that every host adapter is shipped or verified in this repository.

**Pipeline stages and the effective `busdriver.json` routing already determine when and which
agents are used.** Preserve that configuration-driven contract, including its configured fallbacks
and gate-specific restrictions. Hermes selects the pipeline worker; the worker follows the existing
pipeline and resolved routes. Neither gets a new power to improvise reviewer substitutions, skip
stages, or rewrite routing to obtain a PASS. Grok, Agy, Codex, OpenCode and Droid remain helpers or
review backends in this workflow, not additional full-pipeline workers. This revision adds no
free-form scheduler and proposes no migration to Paseo or another orchestrator.

Effective routing at the checkpoint — read from the deployed configs 2026-09-08, **observed**:

| Role | `~/.claude/busdriver.json` (user-global) | `.claude/busdriver.json` (repo) |
|------|------------------------------------------|----------------------------------|
| `litmus.reviewer` | codex → droid | codex → droid |
| `blueprint-review.reviewer_1 / _2 / _3` | agy→droid / codex→droid / grok→droid | same |
| `blueprint-review.auditor` | *(absent — falls back)* | opencode |
| `council.pragmatist / critic / researcher` | agy→droid / codex→droid / grok→droid | same |
| `council.auditor` | *(absent — falls back)* | opencode |
| `auditor.model` | `opencode-go-lb/deepseek-v4-pro` | — |
| `agy_read.model` | `gemini-3.8-flash-high` | — |
| `pi_read.model` | `cursor/auto` | — |
| `ultraOracle.model` | `gpt-5.6-pro` — **BROKEN** | — |

The two files diverge on the auditor routes only. `.claude/CLAUDE.md` still describes the agy-read
lane as "Gemini 3.7 Flash" while the deployed value is `gemini-3.8-flash-high` — a stale factual
claim in contributor docs, **recorded here, not fixed by this revision** (it is a runtime-adjacent
doc edit, out of this document's scope).

**`ultraOracle.model` is not merely drifted — it is broken, and it is the reason this plan's own
review rounds lost their UltraOracle advisory.** `~/.claude/busdriver.json:33-34` sets
`"ultraOracle": { "model": "gpt-5.6-pro" }`, and the run's own error file records verbatim:
*"oracle 0.17.3 … GPT-5.6 Pro is an API reasoning mode, not a model slug. Use `--model gpt-5.6-sol
--reasoning-mode pro`."* A section whose stated purpose is "read from the deployed configs,
observed" should not silently omit the one deployed route that fails. The fix — `gpt-5.6-sol` plus
`--reasoning-mode pro` — is recorded in *Still needs Chris* #8 alongside the Gemini doc drift; both
are the same class of recorded-but-out-of-scope finding, and would otherwise be lost. The advisory
is auxiliary and non-gating, so coverage was never affected.

## Instruction scope — source files are not the delivered context

**2026-09-06 correction, retained.** Busdriver's own `.claude/CLAUDE.md` is contributor guidance for
working on Busdriver. Its presence or size is not evidence that another project receives it by
installing the plugin. Keep self-development and consumer-project audits separate; this distinction
does not make contributor instructions passive prose for item 9.

Three surfaces must never be conflated, and each carries its own evidence grade:

| Surface | What it is | Evidence grade at the checkpoint |
|---------|-----------|-----------------------------------|
| (a) **Self-development** — instructions a session gets while working *on* Busdriver | `~/.claude/CLAUDE.md` + `rules/common/*` + repo `.claude/CLAUDE.md` + the SessionStart orchestrator brief | **observed** — `hooks/hooks.json` registers SessionStart → `load-orchestrator.sh`, which prefers `skills/orchestrator/session-brief.md` over `SKILL.md`; this session's own SessionStart injected that brief |
| (b) **Consumer projects** — what a project loading the plugin actually receives | plugin startup context, skill metadata, on-demand skill bodies | **待核實 (unverified)** — no consumer worktree was inspected in this revision. Record as *unknown*, never as *absent* |
| (c) **External reviewer prompt** — what codex/droid/grok/opencode actually receive | composed by `init-review-loop.sh` → state file → `run-review-loop.sh` | **partial** — the composition path is source-verified and one codex run was observed 2026-09-05; the full composed payload was not retained |

Skill metadata, an on-demand skill body, a script executed without being read, and a reviewer's
composed prompt are different surfaces; do not count every repository file as loaded instructions.
For items 2, 2a, 6 and 10, use a small per-recipient loading map: host/plugin revision, project/CWD,
role/model, instruction source, load trigger, evidence grade. Use redacted traces or content
identifiers and size metadata rather than publishing private prompts or secrets. Only claim context
savings or redundant work from **observed** delivery, reads and execution — not Markdown length or
repeated wording. This is targeted cleanup under existing items, not a new scheduler.

Installed-version drift is itself an instruction-scope fact: the operator machine has plugin
**2.1.14, 2.1.15 and 2.1.16** cached — CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (round-13 finding [16]; UNREVIEWED): an
earlier revision named only the first two. **State the measurement tree once, because this document's credibility
rests on it:** every `file:line` anchor here is verified against the declared checkpoint
`main@727d4652` (**v2.1.15**) with `git show`, not against the working tree. This PR branch is based
on an earlier main and its own tree reads **2.1.14**; the checkpoint is not its ancestor. A gate
observed firing on this host does **NOT identify a version** — CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (round-13 finding
[16]; UNREVIEWED), replacing "a gate observed firing on this host is the *installed* 2.1.15 gate": the
BUILTIN arm sits at the same `:943` in **both** 2.1.15 and 2.1.16, so a firing cannot discriminate them,
and 2.1.16 is the version that actually fired during round 13. Read an observed firing as evidence of
BEHAVIOUR, never of a version number. Where the branch tree and the checkpoint
disagree, the checkpoint is authoritative for every claim in this plan — **and a branch-local
observation MAY still be cited, provided it is REVISION-QUALIFIED** as `<rev>:<path>:<line>` so the tree
it belongs to is explicit; an unqualified anchor is read as the checkpoint's. AMENDED 2026-09-09
(round-13 finding [0]; UNREVIEWED), because the document was already citing both trees and a rule
admitting only one is what let two numberings for a single comparison stand unreconciled. **Pair every
gate-script anchor with its PREDICATE** — the matched pattern, the extraction, the comparison — so a
line shift cannot silently retarget a neighbouring arm. #840 was
written against `main@047a4609`; this revision moves the checkpoint to `main@727d4652`.

## Why this order

Every council voice and witness inverted the audit's cost-first order: a gate set with known
fail-open paths cannot be optimized ("a measurement of theatre"), and policy decisions that say
"decide after data" must schedule their data collection before the changes that would contaminate
the baseline. **That ordering is unchanged and remains approved.**

Items 0, 1 and 5 can proceed in separate worktrees when their edits do not overlap. Capture a
versioned baseline after item 1 before changing prompts, hooks or review policy. Diagnostic logging
may start earlier, but test-polluted or integrity-invalid runs are labelled and excluded from
quality-yield claims. Separate before/after cohorts by code, prompt, policy, harness and routing
revision.
Items 8 and 9 wait for their evidence and the operator decision. Numbers and line references carried
over from 0827 are historical measurements, not current performance targets.

*(An earlier draft added here that "item 2a and item 6 can then proceed while the remaining ledger
sample accumulates". That sentence is **deleted**: it contradicted both the graph's
`baseline captured` rule and item 2a's own blocking edge. The dependency graph is the single ordered
source of truth; this section is a projection of it, not a parallel narrative.)*

> **#840 proposal — REJECTED 2026-09-09 (worker default; the disposition table carries the single
> normative statement, including why Chris #2(i) does not reach it).** #840 rewrites this section to say
> integrity-first means closing a *bounded, evidenced tranche* rather than the whole class, so that
> "an adjacent issue does not automatically freeze unrelated measurement, CI or extraction work."
> That is a real loosening of the approved gate and it is exactly the pressure the original ordering
> was chosen to resist. It is recorded in the disposition table, not applied — and now rejected
> there rather than left pending, on the same no-loosening rule as *Still needs Chris* #3 and #9.
> The approved integrity-first ordering stands unchanged.

## Execution order and acceptance

Status legend — **every status actually used in this table, with its operational meaning; the set is
the bullet list below and the count is derived from it, never written beside it.** An earlier version
defined four and then used three more without definition, and a worker could not tell whether BLOCKED
was a hard stop or a soft preference; a later one said "all seven" while the list already carried
eight — **corrected 2026-09-09 (round 10, propagation pass), by removing the number rather than
updating it**, since a count written next to an enumerated set is the same defect this document
records against "four of the six sites" and item 4's manifest.

- **DONE** — closed, with evidence.
- **IN PROGRESS** — branch activity observed; **assignment unverified** (see *Worker ownership* —
  no signal in this repository establishes assignment, so this never means "a worker owns it").
- **TODO** — schedulable now, subject to the dependency graph.
- **PARTIAL** — named sub-issues remain; do not start new ones without checking *Worker ownership*.
- **BLOCKED** — a **hard stop** on a named predecessor or a named Chris item. Do not start.
- **AUDIT DONE** — analysis complete; **hunks NOT authorised**.
- **SPLIT** — the item is divided into a non-mutating half and a mutating half, **each carrying its
  own status; neither half may be started on the other's authority**. Every SPLIT row must name both
  halves and both gating conditions in its status cell.
- **NEEDS CHRIS** — awaiting an operator decision named in *Still needs Chris*.

The *Progress since the original plan* table copies these values verbatim rather than introducing a
second vocabulary ("not started", "blocked on 2", "audit done, report not on this branch"), so the
two cannot drift. Item text is the 2026-08-27 approved wording; where a #840 amendment was verified
and adopted it is marked **[#840 adopted]** inline.

**GATE-SCRIPT CONTENT LOCK — ONE GLOBAL RULE, DEFINED ONCE HERE, binding on every item in this
table.** `.gate-integrity.lock` pins every file under `hooks/gate-scripts/**` and `scripts/hooks/**`
by SHA-256 (68 pinned paths, verified at `main@727d4652`). **Any commit in this plan that changes a
file under either directory MUST carry the output of `./scripts/gate-integrity.sh --update` in the
SAME reviewed diff.** That is not ordinary CI friction: `.github/workflows/tests.yml:92-108` runs a
dedicated fail-fast `scripts/gate-integrity.sh --check` step and `tests/test-gate-integrity.sh`
asserts the same as its first assertion, so a branch that satisfies every acceptance criterion below
and omits the regen still fails CI. It matters more than that — the lock exists so a gate-body change
cannot reach CI without appearing in the reviewed diff, which is the property this integrity-first
plan is built on. **Items whose named deliverables land inside the locked set** (each cites this rule
rather than restating it) — **RECONCILED against the actual `.gate-integrity.lock` 2026-09-09
(round 10 propagation pass); the regen is required only where a LOCKED path changes, and the
previous wording demanded it in one place where nothing locked is touched:**
- **2a** — `pre-pr-gate.sh:291-313` and `:449-468`, `pre-commit-gate.sh:943-966`,
  `post-commit-consume-marker.sh:154+`. **All three verified present in the lock** (68 entries).
  Regen REQUIRED.
- **2i(B) Stage 2** — the same gate-side set. Regen REQUIRED.
- **2i(B) Stage 1** — **NOT in the locked set, corrected here.** Its named deliverables are the
  producer stamp, the schema/`ALLOWED_TOP` admission and the `_ingest` carry-through, which live in
  `skills/litmus/scripts/` and the backstop schema — **verified absent from the lock**, which pins
  only `hooks/gate-scripts/**` and `scripts/hooks/**`. The earlier "Stage 1 and Stage 2 — the same
  set" made a lock regen part of Stage 1's acceptance when Stage 1 changes no locked file; that is a
  false prerequisite, and a settling check nobody can satisfy honestly is worse than none.
- **7** — `scripts/hooks/pre-read-size-advisory.js`, verified present in the lock. Regen REQUIRED.
  **CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (`全部批准`, round-14 finding [10]; UNREVIEWED):** this bullet also named
  `scripts/hooks/run-with-flags.js` as "the denial transport added by rule (1-b)" — a transport rule
  (1-b) itself withdrew, having established that the launcher is a verbatim pass-through and needs no
  change for the denial. It sits in the locked set (`scripts/hooks/**`) all the same, so its regen is
  required **only if 7b modifies that file for some other reason**, and it is not otherwise a
  deliverable of this row.
- **Item 0 / #622 arrivals** — `hooks/gate-scripts/lib/validate-staged-litmus-marker.sh` and
  `hooks/gate-scripts/lib/merge_pending.py` are named by 2a's membership rule but are **absent from
  this checkout's lock**, because they do not exist here yet; they arrive with #622. The lock
  enumerates from DISK over the whole directory, so they become locked the moment they land, and the
  regen obligation attaches then — **not now**. This is recorded so a worker does not read their
  absence as an exemption.

**Acceptance, stated once for all of them:** `scripts/gate-integrity.sh --check` exits 0 at the
reviewed branch HEAD **for every item whose deliverables include a locked path** — which, per the
list above, excludes 2i(B) Stage 1. Added 2026-09-09 (round 9, finding [9]); none of the three
settling checks named it before.

| # | Item | Class | Status 2026-09-08 | Evidence / settling check |
|---|------|-------|-------------------|---------------------------|
| 0 | **Gate integrity**: #713 (`SHELLOPTS=noexec` in a committed `settings.json` env block silences the outer shell of every contained registration — ADR 0016:210-228 names it as an out-of-scope class residual), #622 (a conflict-free commit reaching a ref without litmus — `pre-commit-gate.sh:182-186` pre-filters on the `commit` token via a `case` on `$HOOK_DATA`, and the `IS_GIT_COMMIT != yes` bail is at `:249` — re-measured 2026-09-08; the 0827 anchors `:112-115`/`:184` no longer hold). **Remaining #622 scope NARROWED 2026-09-08, because the earlier wording claimed a closure the fix does not deliver.** What `fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate` closes is the **multi-parent merge-commit effect** only. Verified on that branch: `merge-reference-transaction-gate.sh:154-156` is `if [[ ! "$OLD" =~ ^0+$ && "$parents" -lt 2 && -n "$FP" && "$FP" == "$OLD" ]]; then exit 0; fi` — every ordinary **single-parent** successor is allowed — and ADR 0051:73-90 states it in terms (**widened from `:73-88` 2026-09-08, round 8, M21** — read on `fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate`, the `git am` quote is at `:81-82` and the cherry-pick/revert `-n` quote at `:89-90`, so the old range cut the second one off): `git am` "is allowed, and is not refused at all", with cherry-pick and revert in scope "via their `-n` two-step form" only. So a plain `git cherry-pick <sha>`, a plain `git revert <sha>`, `git am`, and a sequencer `--continue` of either still land unreviewed. Item 0 does **NOT** close those; they are bucketed to **#783** in *New issues since 2026-08-27* (Ref-gate / #622 spawn). Earlier drafts wrote the scope as "`git merge` / `cherry-pick` / `revert` / `--continue`", which read as if all four were covered. Then the integrity epic: #553, #570, #576, #742, #563. **[#840 adopted]** Reconcile each against merged code and residual ADRs *before* implementing, and name the violated invariant plus the supported threat model for each remaining change; prefer one focused design over a per-review command-spelling exception. | hard | **PARTIAL** — #713, #553, #576, #742, #563 closed. #622 IN PROGRESS. #570 open — 0 commits, but its worktree holds an **uncommitted design doc; do not discard** (see *Worker ownership*). **The two design docs the handover names are absent, but the decisions are not** — #713's design landed as **ADR 0049** (`docs/adr/0049-hook-exec-form-launch-boundary.md`, on main), and #622 carries **ADR 0051** (`docs/adr/0051-native-git-merge-commit-gate.md`) on its own branch, unmerged. The precise gap is therefore *no blueprint-review record covering them*, not "no design exists" — an earlier draft said the latter and was wrong. | `env SHELLOPTS=noexec /bin/sh -c 'echo X'` prints nothing rc 0 (reproduced 2026-08-27; zsh executes). Each fix ships with a test that drives the bypass and asserts the block, exercised through the affected entry point and refused **before** the effect; intended use and recovery still work. Record fixed / still-blocking / owner-accepted residuals separately.<br><br>**Installation verification — added here 2026-09-08, because item 2 declared this clause and item 0 never carried it.** A fix whose enforcement depends on **installed native hooks is NOT complete until installation is verified in the observation repositories**, with the verification command named and its output retained alongside the baseline. ADR 0051:95-98 states the dependency directly: "The gate is only reached where the repository has reference-transaction support and the hook installed; `scripts/install-git-hooks.sh` owns that." A merged PR and an installed plugin version establish nothing about native-hook installation, so establish it by a **READ-ONLY INSTALLED-HOOK AUDIT, per observation repository — corrected 2026-09-08 (round 8, M10), because the clause previously named `scripts/install-git-hooks.sh`'s own digest/pin check and THAT COMMAND DOES NOT EXIST as a check.** Read on `fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate`, the script's argument loop accepts exactly `--force`/`-f` plus one positional path (`:30-36`); there is no `--check`, `--verify` or dry-run mode, and its embedded digest validates gate bytes **as part of INSTALLING**. So the only stated proof that the #622 gate is live was unobtainable as written, and the naive execution **installs or replaces six hooks in every repository the baseline is collected from** — precisely the contamination the *Why this order* section exists to prevent, inflicted on the observation set by the check meant to qualify it. **The audit reads and records the facts enumerated below — (i) through (vii) — and writes nothing** (the lead-in read "three facts" against a five-item list even before (vi) was added; **corrected 2026-09-09, round 10 propagation pass, by deriving the set from the enumeration instead of counting it**): (i) the **effective `core.hooksPath`** as git itself resolves it; (ii) the **presence and executability of all six wrappers**; (iii) **each wrapper's own bytes** — the `exec` target and the two arguments of its embedded digest check, all three chosen by whoever generated it (`scripts/install-git-hooks.sh:338-361` on `fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate`); (iv) the digest of the **live closure**, recomputed over `$PLUGIN_ROOT/hooks/gate-scripts` with the wrapper's own program (`:129-165`) and compared to the digest argument each wrapper carries; (v) the digest of the **snapshot tree each `exec` target lives in**, recomputed the same way and compared to the digest in that snapshot directory's own name; **(vi) the snapshot's gate-script bytes against THIS repository's `.gate-integrity.lock` expectation — ADDED 2026-09-09, approved acceptance strengthening (round 10, finding [17]).** (i)–(v) establish that the installed wrapper is *internally self-consistent*: every digest they compare is one the wrapper itself carries, and both sides are chosen by whoever generated it. A locally-edited or shadowing hook that re-digests its own tree passes all five. Since the whole purpose of this item is to establish that enforcement is ACTIVE where the observations are produced, self-consistency is the wrong proof — it certifies *installation*, not *enforcement of the expected implementation*. (vi) binds the installed exec target to the implementation this repo expects, reusing the primitive `.gate-integrity.lock` already provides rather than inventing a second one; a mismatch DISQUALIFIES that repository from the observation set rather than being recorded as a caveat. It stays read-only and writes nothing, like (i)–(v). (iii)–(vi) are load-bearing, not decoration: the wrapper digests the LIVE tree and then `exec`s `$SNAP/<script>`, where `SNAP="$HOOK_DIR/.busdriver-gates/<digest>.<mkdtemp suffix>"` (`:323`, `:478-552`), so the closure digest authenticates neither the bytes that actually run nor the wrapper that chose them. **Negative case this audit MUST fail on** (stated so the audit is falsifiable rather than a formality): repoint one wrapper's `exec` line at a copy of the gate script with its refusal branch removed, touch nothing under `hooks/gate-scripts`, and leave all six wrappers present and executable — (i), (ii) and (iv) all still pass, and only (iii) and (v) catch it. **(vii) WRAPPER CONTROL FLOW — ADDED 2026-09-09 (round 11), within the approved item-0 acceptance strengthening.** (iii) is scoped to each wrapper's exec target and the two arguments of its embedded digest check — three fields — so the audit authenticates payload bytes and never the wrapper's control flow, which is the thing this item exists to establish. **Each wrapper MUST be compared against the reviewed installer template at `install-git-hooks.sh:338-361` — interpreter, control flow AND arguments, not only the exec target and digest arguments.** `cat` is a RECORDING step and is not a comparison; this requirement names the comparison, which no earlier requirement did. **Second negative case this audit MUST fail on:** a wrapper that keeps the expected exec target and digest arguments but inserts an early `exit 0` above them, with all six wrappers present and executable — it passes (i) through (vi) unchanged while enforcement never runs. The cell's own argument against (i)–(v) — self-consistency certifies installation, not enforcement — applies unchanged to the wrapper itself, and repointing an exec line is a different bypass that does not cover this one. **The verification command, in full** — read-only throughout; still no `--check` mode and still no install: run `git config --get core.hooksPath` and `git rev-parse --git-path hooks`, record BOTH verbatim (including an absent `core.hooksPath`), and if they disagree reconcile by hand and record which was used — this audit must not guess that resolution. Then, over that directory: `ls -l` the six wrapper names, `cat` each wrapper in full (they are ~10 lines), and for the closure plus each distinct snapshot path named by an `exec` line run `python3 -I -S -c "$GATE_DIGEST_PY" <dir>`, with `GATE_DIGEST_PY` taken verbatim from `install-git-hooks.sh:129-165`. **Its output is RETAINED alongside the baseline, per repository per window** — an unretained audit is an assertion, and this row's whole argument is that a gate which cannot be shown live cannot be measured over. Adding a `--check` mode to the script is the alternative and is **not** chosen here: it is a new deliverable on item 0's critical path, where the audit needs no code. This is item 0's requirement, stated in item 0. | 2i | **Pre-baseline integrity preconditions** (new 2026-09-08; the *shared root* of four round-5 HIGH findings). The plan's founding argument is that a gate with known fail-open paths cannot be measured, and it applies that argument to pull **#844** in front of collection. But #844 and item 2a's integrity half were specified *inside* rows whose status is BLOCKED, so the one class of work that must precede all measurement had **no legal start** — and the resulting deadlock is what produced the "standing fallback" that re-opened the bounded-tranche question. This row is that missing stage. It depends on **neither Chris #2 nor item 1**. Two members. **(A) #844 — dedup runs before status and ranks by severity alone.** ([Real open issue](https://github.com/chris-yyau/busdriver/issues/844), opened 2026-09-07.) **Scope it to the general defect, not the scanner instance** — the round-5 arbiter *reproduced* both of these by executing the merger at the checkpoint: an LLM `{high, 0.6}` evicts a matching LLM `{medium, 0.9}` and returns **PASS in both input orders** at `LITMUS_ITERATION=1` (the medium alone returns FAIL), and matching `{high, 0.6}` vs `{high, 0.9}` return **PASS or FAIL purely by argument order** at iterations 1 and 3. Neither case involves a scanner finding, so the earlier "preserve `sast:`/`lint:` findings" repair fixes neither. Fix — **primary prescription corrected AGAIN 2026-09-08 (round 7), because "rank by blocking power with a deterministic tie-break" cannot deliver the order-independence this row's own settling check demands.** Reproduced at the checkpoint: `deduplicate()` (`merge-findings.py:20-41`) is a greedy single pass over a mutable `seen` list which, on a win, does `seen.remove(s); seen.append(f)` — moving the survivor to the END and so changing the comparison order for every later finding — and the match predicate is `SequenceMatcher` ratio `> 0.6`, which is **not transitive**. With three same-file same-line findings whose pairwise similarities are A~B 0.921, A~C 0.653, B~C 0.587, a **fully deterministic** tie-break (severity rank, then confidence) still yields **THREE distinct surviving sets across the 6 input permutations** — `{B}`, `{B,C}` and `{A,B}`. A tie-break is necessary and demonstrably not sufficient; the order-dependence lives in the greedy traversal, not in the comparison. **So: replace the construction, do not re-rank it.** **First make the edge relation symmetric, then cluster.** `SequenceMatcher(None, a, b).ratio()` is **not symmetric**, so the graph's own edges depend on comparison order and no clustering over it can be order-independent. Measured 2026-09-08 on the chain fixture below: `A→B = 0.608108` but `B→A = 0.567568`, which **straddles the 0.6 threshold** — the A–B edge exists or does not exist depending purely on which finding the merger reaches first (`A→C = 0.428571` vs `C→A = 0.396825` is asymmetric too, though it does not cross). **Required: compare each pair in a canonical order — sort the two NORMALIZED descriptions (the lower-cased text the merger already compares) lexicographically and always call `SequenceMatcher` on `(lo, hi)`.** Canonical ordering is specified because it is deterministic and costs one comparison rather than a second `SequenceMatcher` call; `max(fwd, rev)` and `min(fwd, rev)` are also order-independent, but they are **different clusterings** — on the measured fixture `min` retains a finding the other two elect away — so the symmetrization must be NAMED, not left to the implementer. Any agreement between canonical and `max` on a particular fixture is a property of that fixture, **not** a general equivalence. **Next, state the EDGE PREDICATE in full — it is not description similarity alone. Corrected 2026-09-08 (round 8); an earlier version of this clause said only "build the similarity graph over ALL findings" and specified only the `SequenceMatcher` comparison.** Verified at the checkpoint, `deduplicate()` reaches `SequenceMatcher` only after two guards: `f.get("file") == s.get("file")` and `abs(f.get("line", 0) - s.get("line", 0)) <= 3` (`merge-findings.py:26-27`). Those guards are **requirements of this row**, not incidental incumbent behaviour that an implementer may infer: an edge exists **iff** same `file` **AND** `abs(line delta) <= 3` **AND** canonical-order `SequenceMatcher` ratio `> 0.6`. Read without them, this prescription merges findings in *unrelated files* — a strictly larger over-merge than any ordering defect, and one this row's same-file same-line fixtures cannot detect. Measured 2026-09-08 by executing both readings: two findings at canonical ratio `0.972`, one in `app/a.py` and one in `app/b.py`, are collapsed to a single survivor by the description-only reading and kept separate by the full predicate. **Add a negative control to the acceptance matrix: near-identical descriptions in two different files, asserted NOT merged.**<br><br>**One large scanner-output benchmark is also part of this acceptance matrix — added 2026-09-08 (round 8, M23).** Graph construction is all-pairs and is **not** bounded by the LLM reviewer's 10-issue budget, because scanner findings enter the merger as well — **and that is consistent with the exclusion clause above, not a contradiction of it, which the row now says explicitly (clarified 2026-09-09, round 10, finding [19])**: the merger's deterministic-source exclusion is scoped to **blocking severities**, so `sast:`/`lint:` findings at **high or medium** are kept out of the similarity graph, while **low-severity scanner findings enter it normally** and count toward the all-pairs bound. Two different populations, one sentence each — read the exclusion as "not blocking-severity scanner findings", never as "no scanner findings". Measured at the checkpoint, **499 comparisons ≈ 10 ms** against **124,750 ≈ 2.6 s** for 500 findings, with `SequenceMatcher` itself quadratic per comparison. **The rationale first given for calling it non-blocking is WITHDRAWN — it was refuted by execution 2026-09-09 (round 9, finding [23]).** That rationale said the two guards run before any `SequenceMatcher` call "which is what keeps the real cost near the 10 ms figure". Measured through the guards: 500 findings at the SAME file and line pass BOTH guards for all **124,750** pairs — **124,750 `SequenceMatcher` calls, 4.81 s**; 500 across distinct files, **0 calls / 5 ms**; 500 in one file with spread lines, **0 calls / 8 ms**. The guards bound the cost only when findings are SPREAD and bound it **not at all** in a dense bucket — which is both this row's own #844 fixture shape (same file, same line) and the normal shape of scanner output at one location. The benchmark is **retained**, and it is still recorded low and non-blocking, but on the honest ground: the quadratic cost is real and unbounded in the dense case, and this row accepts it rather than explains it away. A separate correction from the same finding: the benchmark must use **non-deterministic** findings, since the **source-prefix exclusion stated once above** — cited here, not restated, and no longer paraphrased with a severity scope it does not have (**CORRECTED 2026-09-10, round-15 finding [2]; UNREVIEWED**) — keeps **every** `sast:`/`lint:` finding out of the graph regardless of severity, so a 500-SAST-finding benchmark would exercise nothing. **Add one passthrough control alongside it:** a valid `low` `sast:` finding duplicated in shape, asserted to survive with **no similarity comparison performed on it at all** — that is what distinguishes true exclusion from a rank that merely happens to win.<br><br>**Then EXCLUDE deterministic blockers from dedup entirely — do not merely rank them. Corrected 2026-09-08 (round 8), because the election key as previously written reintroduced the very #844 false-PASS this row exists to close.** Verified at the checkpoint: `_is_deterministic_blocker` (`:45-49`) returns True for any `sast:`/`lint:` finding at high or medium, and `determine_status` returns FAIL on it **first** (`:70-71`), *before* the iteration filter at `:72-73`. The previous key placed `deterministic_source` **fourth**, behind `normalized_confidence`, so a matching `sast:` medium at confidence `0.4` and an `llm:` medium at `0.99` tie on the first two elements and the LLM finding wins on confidence — the deterministic element never runs. Measured 2026-09-08 over all input permutations at `LITMUS_ITERATION=3`: the undeduped truth is **FAIL** (the SAST medium blocks regardless of iteration); the incumbent returns **FAIL or PASS by input order**; and the previously prescribed key returns **PASS deterministically, in every order** — converting today's order-dependent coin-flip into a reliable false PASS, which is worse than the defect it replaces. So: deterministic findings — **identified by SOURCE PREFIX (`sast:` / `lint:`) ALONE, independently of the incumbent severity membership test** — are **never candidates for collapse and never collapsed into**; they always survive to `issues[]`. **RE-SCOPED 2026-09-10 (`全部批准`, round-14 finding [14]; UNREVIEWED).** The rule previously inherited its scope from `_is_deterministic_blocker`, which is a **CONJUNCTION** — verified at HEAD, `merge-findings.py:45-49`: `severity in ("high", "medium") and (source.startswith("sast:") or source.startswith("lint:"))`. A `sast:` or `lint:` finding whose severity is ABSENT, `null` or merely miscased `"HIGH"` therefore failed the first conjunct, was never excluded, and remained an ordinary collapse candidate — so the rule protected strictly fewer findings than it appeared to, and the ones it silently dropped are exactly the malformed records item 8 requires to block. Keying the exclusion on the source prefix closes that gap and touches no blocking predicate. **TWO THINGS THIS DOES NOT DO, named because each would be new policy and neither is authorized here:** it does **not** make a VALID `low` `sast:` finding blocking — such a finding becomes protected from collapse and still does not block, leaving the incumbent severity policy exactly as it stands, iteration-3 mediums included; and it does **not** move, re-time or re-place item 8's validation, which stays precisely where its row puts it. A malformed `sast:`/`lint:` record is handled there, as a blocking validation failure on the same footing as any other malformed record, and never by a severity test at this layer. Only then: build the similarity graph over the **remaining (non-deterministic) findings**, cluster it, and elect **one representative per cluster** by a **total order stated here rather than by reference to "blocking power"**, since that phrase is undefined where neither twin blocks and would otherwise make the surviving set iteration-dependent. Three properties of that construction are specified below because each was found defective by execution rather than by reading; **all three corrected 2026-09-08 (round 8, findings M6/M7/M8), and none of the three is optional.**<br><br>**(α) MEMBERSHIP — collapse only DIRECT duplicates of the elected representative, never everything reachable from it.** Connected components perform **transitive closure** over a predicate that is not transitive, so a finding that matches *nothing in the surviving set* is dropped because some third finding shared wording with both it and the representative. A member is collapsed **iff it satisfies the duplicate predicate against the ELECTED REPRESENTATIVE itself**; a reachable non-duplicate survives as its own representative. **RESTATED 2026-09-09 (round 9, finding [17]) as ONE order-independent loop, because "cluster, then elect one representative per cluster" is inconsistent with this property for any cluster of diameter ≥ 2 and left leftovers unspecified — on a four-node chain A-B-C-D, C and D both survive AND are direct duplicates of each other, so the output held an uncollapsed duplicate pair while the row claimed one representative per cluster. THE ALGORITHM: sort all non-deterministic candidates DESCENDING by the total key; take the head as a representative; drop any later candidate matching an already-kept representative under the full edge predicate (same `file`, `abs(line delta) <= 3`, canonical-order `SequenceMatcher > 0.6`); repeat until none remain. No separate clustering step, no leftovers, and (α)'s direct-match property is preserved exactly. Fixture: the four-node chain, asserting no two survivors are direct duplicates, over all 24 permutations.** Measured 2026-09-08 on this row's own chain fixture (`A~B = 0.608`, `B~C = 0.800`, `A~C = 0.397`, with the election landing on the **endpoint** A): components return `{A}` and **erase C**, while representative-checked membership returns `{A, C}`. **The trade is stated here rather than left to the implementer:** the incumbent `deduplicate()` (`:20-42`) compares each candidate only against kept survivors and so already drops on a direct match alone — this rule preserves that property while removing the ordering defect, and it deliberately accepts a **larger** surviving set than components would produce. **Silent loss is the specific hazard:** on the measured fixture the status is FAIL either way, so nothing in the verdict signals that a finding disappeared.<br><br>**(β) THE KEY MUST BE TOTAL, on both grounds it was not.** The order is `(blocks_at_iteration_1, severity_rank, normalized_confidence, deterministic_source, source, description, file, line, category, suggestion)`, compared in that order, **and TERMINATED by a canonical serialization of the complete validated record** — `json.dumps(finding, sort_keys=True, separators=(',',':'))` — so the key is total by construction rather than by enumeration. **THAT CLAIM WAS FALSE FOR EVERY COMPONENT BUT ONE, and is corrected here 2026-09-09 (round 10, finding [8]) — refuted by EXECUTION, not by reading.** The terminator makes the key total only among records whose earlier components all *compare*; it is never reached if an earlier comparison raises. β-1 below supplies a sentinel for `normalized_confidence` alone, while `source`, `description`, `file`, `line`, `category` and `suggestion` are read straight off the finding dicts, where an optional key is simply absent. Running the key exactly as specified: two records identical through `category`, one carrying `suggestion` and one not, raise `TypeError: '<' not supported between instances of 'NoneType' and 'str'`; the same with `line` absent raises `NoneType` vs `int`. **The precise trigger is ASYMMETRIC PRESENCE** — one record has the field and the other does not, with every earlier component equal; when both omit it the tuple compares fine, which is why reading the spec does not reveal it and why a fixture where both sides are well-formed will never catch it. This is the ordinary two-reviewer case, not an exotic one: reviewers differ in which optional fields they emit. **REQUIRED: every component passes through a typed accessor with an explicit per-component sentinel before it enters the tuple — empty string for `source`, `description`, `file`, `category` and `suggestion`; a numeric floor for `line` — **whose value is **`-1`** and which is the SORT SENTINEL ONLY, never the dedup edge predicate — VALUE FIXED HERE 2026-09-09 (`全部批准`, round-14 finding [15]; UNREVIEWED)**, replacing "whose VALUE and semantics are settled below": no concrete value was settled below, or anywhere else, so the one surface that promised it deferred to nothing and every downstream fixture was unwritable. **`-1` is chosen for two reasons and both are checkable:** it sorts strictly below every `line` a reviewer can emit, a file-level `0` included; and it is deliberately NOT the edge predicate's `:27` default of `0`, so the two constants can never be read as one shared value — which is the confusion (β) exists to prevent. It is this list's entry and nothing derives from it; the β-1 sentinel for `normalized_confidence`; `False` for the two booleans; and **`severity_rank`** — under the rank map stated once here, which is the INCUMBENT map, reused rather than reinvented: **`high` 3, `medium` 2, `low` 1, and anything unrecognized (including an absent or `None` severity) 0**, which is also its sentinel. Never a bare `None` in any position**<br><br>**THE `line` FLOOR AND THE DEDUP EDGE ARE TWO SEPARATE THINGS — CHRIS-APPROVED 2026-09-09 (`全部批准`, round-13 finding [11]); PLAN TEXT ONLY, UNREVIEWED, and BEHAVIOUR-CHANGING, so it lands behind this row's existing gate alongside the rest of the merger work.** Conflating them is what made the floor's value load-bearing in two incompatible ways. **(a) SORT SENTINEL — ordering only.** `line` absent or `None` sorts as a fixed numeric floor of **`-1`** — the value fixed once beside the other sentinels in the typed-accessor list above, cited here and not restated. It is safe to fix precisely because it never reaches the edge predicate, and it is distinct from that predicate's `0` default so the two are never conflated. **(b) EDGE PREDICATE — dedup only, and NOT a numeric comparison.** The incumbent edge is `abs(f.get("line", 0) - s.get("line", 0)) <= 3` (`merge-findings.py:27`), so under a `0` floor an UNLOCATED finding (`line` absent or `None`) matches **every** finding at lines 0–3 in the same file — collapsing distinct defects and letting the election at `:36-38` discard the loser. **The rule: an UNLOCATED finding is a candidate ONLY against another UNLOCATED finding in the same file; a located finding is a candidate only against another LOCATED finding within 3 lines. Located and unlocated NEVER match each other, at any floor value.** Locatedness is therefore part of the predicate, not something a default smuggles in. **PIN BOTH DIRECTIONS** so neither half can silently invert: (i) two UNLOCATED findings in the same file with descriptions over the `0.6` ratio **DO** collapse, and the election still elects by `severity_rank`; (ii) one UNLOCATED and one LOCATED finding in the same file, descriptions identical, **DO NOT** collapse and both are retained — asserted in both input orders, since the incumbent predicate is order-independent and any replacement must stay so. (the enumeration previously covered nine of the ten components and omitted `severity_rank` while the same sentence demanded one per component; **corrected 2026-09-09, round 11**). **POLARITY CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (round 12) — this is the third round on this prescription, and the two earlier ones were wrong in the same direction.** Round 11 wrote `high` 0, `medium` 1, `low` 2 with the sentinel as "the rank of the LOWEST severity". Under the DESCENDING sort this algorithm specifies, that is inverted twice over: `2 > 1 > 0` elects `low` over `medium` over `high`, and a sentinel of 2 sorts an unknown severity FIRST rather than last. **The map above is the one the incumbent already uses** — `skills/litmus/scripts/lib/merge-findings.py:35` builds `{"high": 3, "medium": 2, "low": 1}` and compares with `sev_rank.get(...) > sev_rank.get(...)` at `:36`, whose `.get(..., 0)` default places an unrecognized severity BELOW `low`. Descending order and severity now agree, and the sentinel sorts last. **Note for the implementer: that map is FUNCTION-LOCAL to `deduplicate()` and is not an importable module symbol** — the diagnostic below therefore asserts on observable behaviour, and any implementation that wants to share the map must first hoist it, in the same reviewed diff. **The repair is NOT confined to the ordering key — corrected 2026-09-09 (round 11).** `merge-findings.py:27` computes the duplicate predicate `abs(f.get("line", 0) - s.get("line", 0)) <= 3` on RAW fields, and `.get("line", 0)` returns its default only when the key is ABSENT: a record carrying `line: null` yields `None`, `None - 0` raises `TypeError`, and the sort-key repair never touches it. **So the ORDERING AND DEDUPLICATION consumers take the same typed accessor — and ONLY those. A permissive sentinel in the election can therefore retire a malformed authorization-bearing record that the excluded readers would have blocked, which is the same fail-open the exclusion exists to prevent, reached one frame earlier. **The gap is closed in item 8, not here:** item 8's fail-closed validation rejects such a record at the merger's ingestion interface, BEFORE `:185`, so no unvalidated record ever reaches this election. how page boundaries bind to `reviewed_diff_hash`; and how `merge-findings.py` combines across pages, given that its dedup is description-similarity based.<br><br>**Status of that transport, stated honestly 2026-09-08: it is NOT specified here.** The five clauses above are the questions the transport must answer, not the answers — this row sets the bar "specify the TRANSPORT before mandating fixtures" and then mandates three fixtures against a transport it has not specified, so an engineer reaching this row cannot implement it. **Choosing the envelope is a design decision, and it is left open deliberately rather than invented in a plan-only revision**; its length is derived, never restated):** (1) accumulating arbitrary review-shaped objects would **destroy the extractor's existing ability to treat a later object as a CORRECTED verdict that replaces**, so the paged and un-paged cases must be distinguishable — a page must be self-identifying, and an object without that identification must keep today's replace semantics; (2) continuation within a single iteration must carry a **hard page cap**, or overflow becomes a way to keep reviewing past the iteration-based stopping limits; (3) dedup must run **across pages after accumulation**, not per page, or the merger's own similarity matching never sees the duplicates it exists to collapse. **(4) Cap exhaustion is a THIRD terminal outcome, distinct from both `complete` and `truncated` — decided 2026-09-08, because three of this row's own requirements could not hold simultaneously at the cap.** With the 10-issue page budget verified at the checkpoint (`init-review-loop.sh:464`/`:554`) and a finite page cap P, a review carrying more than 10P findings could only omit one — the laundering this row exists to remove — or mark itself `truncated`, which blocks a review whose every omitted finding may have been advisory. The resolution is neither: such a review is stamped `cap_exhausted`, which is **non-authorizing and requires SCOPE REDUCTION** (a smaller diff, a split commit), and which is **approved as an operational limit rather than a defect to be engineered away**. **The cost is stated rather than hidden: an advisory-only review CAN fail to authorize at the cap.** The unqualified promise that eleven advisory findings never block holds only **within** the cap; beyond it, unreportability withdraws authorization regardless of severity. **(5) OVERALL DEADLINE — ACCEPTED 2026-09-09 (Chris, round-13 finding [12]); UNREVIEWED.** Any transport must specify a single OVERALL deadline spanning all continuation pages, retries and fallback attempts — or an explicitly supported DETACHED execution lifecycle not bounded by the caller's timeout — and must define the outcome of a deadline hit MID-CONTINUATION. Per-page budgets do not compose, and the hazard is already verified at the checkpoint, which is this list's own admission criterion: `run-review-loop.sh:3254` sets `REVIEW_TIMEOUT="${LITMUS_TIMEOUT:-540}"` explicitly *"UNDER the 600s harness Bash cap"*, `:1134` carries the same `:-540` for the PR backstop, and `:1329` already records that a 3-attempt sequence *"could reach 3x the timeout plus backoff -- past the 600s"* cap; page dispatches each carrying a fresh budget reproduce that same killed-process failure under any finite page cap P, and a process killed mid-continuation has no defined outcome under the clauses above. A deadline hit mid-continuation **RETAINS the findings captured so far** and yields a **NON-AUTHORIZING incomplete** outcome — `truncated`, or `cap_exhausted` per (4) — **never a clean verdict**. ACCEPTANCE: a fixture in which the deadline fires after the first page retains that page's findings and does not authorize. **FIXTURES — BINDING ONLY ONCE THE TRANSPORT ENVELOPE IS APPROVED.** This row's own bar is "specify the TRANSPORT before mandating fixtures", and the transport is a named deliverable of the convergence-policy half, which is BLOCKED on `baseline captured` (its *Still needs Chris* #2 conjunct is SATISFIED — #2 ANSWERED 2026-09-09; the label is corrected here 2026-09-09, round 10, finding [13], because naming an answered row as a live gate reads as a phantom approval dependency). So the four below are a **specification of what the chosen envelope must make testable**, not schedulable work, and they are explicitly NOT in the same binding tense as this row's other acceptance criteria, which ARE executable today. Nothing is delayed by this, because the transport gates them anyway (marker added 2026-09-09, round 9, finding [25]). The four: **eleven advisory findings, which must NOT block**; **a blocking finding beyond the first page, which MUST block**; the reverse case the current extractor makes possible — **a blocking first page followed by a clean final page, which must still block**; and **a cap-plus-one advisory fixture** — one advisory finding more than the hard cap can carry — which must yield `cap_exhausted`, must NOT authorize, and must name scope reduction as the remedy. **(End of the envelope-conditional fixtures; what follows is binding now.)** Neither omission nor iteration advancement may ever **GRANT** an authorization that the undeduped, uncapped review would not (the earlier wording said "change the authorization outcome", which cap exhaustion contradicts by design — it WITHDRAWS one). | hard; split around the baseline | **SPLIT** — **integrity half** is **item 2i**: its scope is the **2i NODE**, defined once in the dependency graph and **not restated here**. **The envelope/provenance work of bounds (i-a) and (i-b) is NOT in that node** — it is the **`2a-env`** node, a sibling ahead of `baseline captured`, added 2026-09-09 (round 11) because (i-b) changes an authorization outcome, which (B) Stage 1 forbids by its own stated rule while (B) Stage 2 — the member that may change one — sits downstream of `baseline captured`; its gate and acceptance are the graph's (**re-cited 2026-09-09, round 12**: this said "the 2i NODE forbids that", a node-wide prohibition the same round's per-member correction removed). This is not a loophole: rule (1)'s only exemption is a line `limit` no greater than the threshold, and a line limit is meaningless for these formats — the Claude Code `Read` tool is the documented way to open **images** (no line concept whatsoever) and **PDFs** (paginated by a `pages` parameter, not `offset`/`limit`). **The exemption is decided on the tool's DECLARED FORMAT HANDLING, BEFORE any line-count evaluation — corrected 2026-09-08 (round 8, M13), because keying it on `:99` is false for PDFs and the failure lands OUTSIDE rule (4) entirely.** PNG always trips `:99` (the IHDR length field is `00 00 00 0D` immediately after the signature) and JPEG effectively always does (segment length high bytes), but **PDF need not**: the format permits entirely uncompressed, human-readable object and content streams, and a **5471-byte NUL-free PDF was measured as 237 lines** by the checkpoint's own `countLines`. Such a file returns a **number above the threshold**, so it never reaches rule (4) at all — it is denied by **rule (1)** as an ordinary oversized text read, whose only exemption is a line `limit` that the `Read` tool does not apply to PDFs (they are paginated by `pages`). What survives from round 11 is only the observation that prompted the kind: `:234-239` returns a bare `{ exitCode: 0 }` on a trust-ROOT establishment failure (`fs.realpathSync(cwd)` / `fs.statSync`), and no file-outcome kind could represent it; **(b)** `:224-226` is NOT a probe outcome at all: under (1-a) its two halves are DISPOSITIONS, and each carries the kind of the branch it routes to — the path-safety half an allow, the range half the disposition table. **The contract therefore closes at the membership the canonical table carries**, and the count is derived by reading that table and is written nowhere — **the bare figure that stood here is struck 2026-09-09 (`批准840`, round-14 finding [11]; UNREVIEWED)**, since a count written beside a list is exactly how this row came to declare one number and enumerate another. **`not_found` is also SPLIT OUT of `probe_unavailable`:** a file that is not there is not a probe that failed, and the two must not share a disposition. **THE SITE IS CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (round 12) — the earlier justification named a mechanism that does not exist.** It claimed `resolveContained`'s catch at `:74-76` swallows ENOENT together with a genuine containment error. The two are different states and the row does not merge them. **The cost is ACCEPTED, not overlooked:** on any host that is neither darwin nor linux (`:138`), and intermittently on macOS whenever `/usr/sbin/lsof` is slow or fails (`:126`, `:130`, `:140`, and `:158` downstream of them), an unbounded `Read` is denied where the incumbent allowed it — a **new prohibition** over a probe that today never denies anything. This is a deliberate fail-closed posture on a **cost** gate, chosen in full sight of that lockout. **Fixtures — deny:** a simulated `lsof` non-zero exit, and a non-darwin/non-linux platform; both must DENY and both must offer the `limit` recovery in the block message. Rationale — this repo's doctrine is that an enforcement gate which cannot verify its condition blocks, and a *cost* gate is uniquely cheap to satisfy under that rule, since the agent clears it by passing a `limit`, which is meaningful for exactly these cases. This does **not** contradict rule (2): a hook-**launch** failure is the gate not running at all (fail-open, unchanged), whereas an indeterminate probe is the gate running and reaching no conclusion. | 8 | Litmus commit mode: keep `medium` blocking. Stratified sample of 20 medium-only FAILs with predeclared thresholds, dispositioned against the item-2 ledger, **AND a separately adjudicated stratum of iteration-3-and-later medium-only LLM PASSes — ACCEPTED 2026-09-09 (Chris, round-13 finding [13]); UNREVIEWED.** A FAIL-only frame excludes the outcomes the decision would change: `blocking_severities` is `{high, medium}` for iterations 1–2 and `{high}` from iteration 3 (`merge-findings.py:64-67`, byte-identical at HEAD and the checkpoint), so a valid medium-only LLM review FAILs early and PASSes from iteration 3 — sampling FAILs alone therefore draws almost entirely from iterations 1–2 plus deterministic scanner findings, and omits precisely the advisory-treated cases whose authorization behaviour "keep `medium` blocking" would actually alter. **How each stratum bears on the decision is PREDECLARED**, alongside the thresholds, not chosen once the counts are visible. Within both strata two separations are required: **deterministic (`sast:`/`lint:`) findings are counted apart from LLM findings** — they block at every iteration via `_is_deterministic_blocker` (`:45-49`, invoked at `:70-71`) and so cannot discriminate this policy — and **repeated observations of ONE defect are counted apart from distinct defects**, so frequency is not mistaken for impact. Then decide. CodeScene: one final advisory state in the ack ledger, drop per-event acks — re-opens ADR 0012 (`:37`) explicitly; ONE umbrella CodeScene-debt issue absorbing #759 #733 #725 #716 #700 #637 #590 #564 (no blanket close). **[#840 adopted]** Pin the **whole** `determine_status` contract in tests first, not just the iteration rule: SAST/lint high+medium always block — cite this precisely as **`merge-findings.py:45-49` (`_is_deterministic_blocker`), invoked as the first branch of `determine_status` at `:70-71`**. (`:49` is the helper's `return`, not a branch inside `determine_status`, which begins at `:52`; a test author following the old anchor looks in the wrong function.) **Pin it at the merger's stdin/argv interface, not at `determine_status`** — the helper is correct in isolation and is defeated upstream at `:185`. Beyond that: an LLM finding blocks only when its severity is in the iteration set **and** its normalized confidence is ≥ 0.7; the iteration set is `{high, medium}` for iterations 1–2 and `{high}` from iteration 3 (`LITMUS_ITERATION`, `:64`, `:67`). **Correction — the "missing or non-numeric confidence blocks unconditionally" clause in an earlier draft was false, and implementing it would have re-blocked iteration-3 mediums through the missing-confidence path (exactly the item-8 / item-2a collision this row exists to avoid).** The code's actual predicate: `:70-71` returns FAIL for deterministic blockers; `:72-73` is `if f.get("severity","") not in blocking_severities: continue`; **only then**, at `:75-82`, is confidence inspected. So a missing or non-numeric confidence FAILs **only for findings whose severity already survived the iteration filter**. Pin both directions: a **high** LLM finding with `confidence: null` FAILs at every iteration; a **medium** LLM finding with `confidence: null` remains **PASS at iteration 3**.<br><br>**EXPLICIT FAIL-CLOSED VALIDATION OF THE AUTHORIZATION-BEARING FIELDS — CHRIS-APPROVED 2026-09-09 (`全部批准`, round-13 finding [5]); PLAN TEXT ONLY, UNREVIEWED, and BEHAVIOUR-CHANGING, so it lands with the rest of this row BEHIND item 8's existing gate — it is NOT startable early and does NOT move into any outcome-neutral surface.** Measured against the checkpoint before writing (`determine_status`, `:52-88`, byte-identical at HEAD): the guard at `:72-73` is `if f.get("severity","") not in blocking_severities: continue`, so a finding the gate cannot classify is **skipped rather than blocked**. Four fail-opens are reachable today, each verified by execution at `LITMUS_ITERATION=1`: a finding with **severity ABSENT** returns PASS; **`severity: null`** returns PASS; an **unrecognized** severity returns PASS — including the mere miscasing **`"HIGH"`**, which no schema forbids; and a `high` finding with a **non-finite `confidence` of `NaN`** returns PASS, because `nan >= 0.7` is False and `json.loads` accepts the bare `NaN` literal. **The deliverable:** each of those four is a **validation failure that BLOCKS**, not a finding to skip — the gate must never authorize over input it could not classify.<br><br>**THE VALIDATION RUNS OVER ALL INGESTED RECORDS, BEFORE `deduplicate()` — CHRIS-APPROVED 2026-09-09 (`批准840`, round-14 finding [0]); PLAN TEXT ONLY, UNREVIEWED.** The ordering is load-bearing, not an implementation detail. `main()` calls `:185 deduped = deduplicate(all_findings)` and only then `:186 status = determine_status(deduped)`, so a record eliminated by the dedup election at `:36-38` is unobservable to any validation placed inside `determine_status` — the four fail-opens above would be closed for a record that arrives alone and left open for the same record arriving as a duplicate. Measured against the checkpoint-identical worktree merger before writing: a duplicate pair of one valid `medium` (`a.js:10`, `confidence: 0.9`) and one **severity-ABSENT** twin (`a.js:11`, same description, over the `0.6` ratio) returns **PASS at `LITMUS_ITERATION=3` in BOTH input orders**, the malformed record dropped by the election either way; the malformed record ALONE at the same iteration also returns PASS, and the iteration-1 control FAILs on the valid `medium`. So downstream of `:185` the malformed record cannot be blocked, or even counted. **The validation therefore sits at the merger's stdin/argv INGESTION point, ahead of `:185`, and rejects on the record as ingested; a record that fails validation is a blocking validation failure and never enters the election.** It is not a `determine_status` guard and must not be implemented as one — which is the same placement the `[#840 adopted]` paragraph above already requires for the deterministic-blocker contract, stated there for a different reason and cited here rather than re-argued. **Two paths are ALREADY fail-closed and must be preserved, not re-implemented:** `confidence: None` returns FAIL at `:76-78`, and an unparseable `confidence` returns FAIL at `:80-81`; positive infinity also FAILs, incidentally, via the `> 1.0` normalization. **The policy for a VALID `medium` is UNCHANGED** — blocking at iterations 1–2, advisory from iteration 3 — and a fixture pins that explicitly so the tightening cannot be mistaken for a severity-policy change. **Acceptance:** six fixtures, four asserting FAIL (absent, null, unrecognized-including-miscased, NaN) and two asserting the incumbent behaviour of the valid `medium` at iteration 1 (FAIL) and iteration 3 (PASS). **A SEVENTH FIXTURE PINS THE ORDERING — ADDED 2026-09-09 (`批准840`, round-14 finding [0]; UNREVIEWED):** the malformed-vs-valid DUPLICATE pair measured above — one valid `medium` and one severity-ABSENT twin, same `file`, `line` within 3, descriptions over the `0.6` ratio — driven through the merger's ingestion interface and asserted to **FAIL at `LITMUS_ITERATION=3` in BOTH input orders**. Both orders are required because the election is order-sensitive at `:36-38` while the defect is not, and iteration 3 is required because it is the iteration at which the valid `medium` alone PASSes — so the only thing that can produce a FAIL is the malformed record actually being validated. It is the one fixture of the seven that fails if the validation is placed after `:185`, and the measured incumbent PASS in both orders is its negative baseline. Quote `:70-82` beside `:67` in the test so the author implements the code, not the prose. Cover the 0–1 vs 0–100 normalization branch, run reinitialisation, and both commit/PR modes — **verified on `origin/main`**. Note what the source itself says: the iteration relaxation is a deliberate **server-side override of the prompt contract**, which still tells the LLM to fail on any medium. | 12 | Adversarial end-to-end pipeline-integrity suite (hostile committed env, shell expansion, merge commands, diff drivers, launcher substitution, missing CI shards). **[#840 adopted — strictly stronger]** The minimum regression for each item-0 fix is part of **that fix's acceptance**, not deferred behind the whole roadmap; the broader families expand from there. **State the disposition per in-flight item-0 branch, or this strengthened rule binds only unowned future work.** *Worker ownership* says do not re-plan #622, #780 and #781 — all active item-0 branches (29, 36 and 36 commits ahead) — and the plan never said whether they must absorb the item-12 matrix (hostile committed env, git aliases, `GIT_*` redirection via a settings `env` block, `pull --ff-only`, sequencer `--continue`) **before merge**, or are grandfathered under their own tests. One explicit sentence each is required: either "before merge, this branch must include the item-12 minimum cases for the invariant it claims to close", or "grandfathered; item-12 cases land as follow-up issue #NNN", naming the issue. **The three sentences, supplied here rather than demanded — an earlier revision stated the requirement in imperative form and then left every row blank.** Read from `git show fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate:docs/adr/0051-native-git-merge-commit-gate.md` without checking the branch out.<br>— **`fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate` (29 ahead): before merge, this branch must include the item-12 minimum cases for the invariant it claims to close.** The enumeration is executable now and turns on ADR 0051's central choice: it enforces on the **effect** — refusing a branch-tip update to a merge commit unless a pending claim binds that exact `HEAD`, staged tree and `MERGE_HEAD` set — at git's own `reference-transaction` `prepared` phase, *not* on command spelling. That covers the command-shape family **whose effect is a merge commit** — conflict-free `git merge`, aliases via `-c alias.x`, last-wins option overrides, and compound commands — because each must ultimately move a ref to a MULTI-PARENT commit. **An earlier revision of this sentence said it "structurally covers the whole command-shape family the historical handover enumerates". That is false and is corrected here**, on the same evidence item 0 now carries: `merge-reference-transaction-gate.sh:154-156` exits 0 for every single-parent successor, and ADR 0051:73-90 (widened from `:73-88`, round 8 M21) allows `git am` outright and takes cherry-pick/revert only in their `-n` two-step form. It does **NOT cover**: (a) **fast-forward / `pull --ff-only`** — stated precisely, because "the merge-commit predicate never fires" overstated it: a single-commit FF with `FP == OLD` exits 0 at `:154-156`, and a multi-commit FF is adjudicated by the witnessed-FF rule at `:474-489`. Either way no merge commit is created, so the governing invariant is ADR 0050's ref-ff gate, a separate control (#779/#780), not this branch; (b) **hook-location redirection** via `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_COMMON_DIR` **only**, which relocates the operation into a repository whose hooks are not this one's — an installation residual. **`GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` are NOT in this bucket, and grouping them here was wrong (corrected 2026-09-08).** Only `GIT_DIR`/`GIT_COMMON_DIR` change the git directory and therefore the hooks directory; `GIT_INDEX_FILE` selects an alternate index **within the same repository**, and `GIT_WORK_TREE` changes the working tree without selecting a different git dir — neither moves the hooks. The distinction is load-bearing for this plan specifically: alternate-index binding is part of the claimed authorization invariant (no invocation may replace the index or tree the marker was bound to), so **`GIT_INDEX_FILE` and `GIT_WORK_TREE` belong INSIDE the gate's own predicate as index/tree-binding cases the gate must either bind to or refuse**, not outside it as an installation residual. Cite git's environment-variables documentation and `githooks`' location rules in the test so a later reader does not re-collapse them; (c) **sequencer/`rebase`/`am` paths that produce non-merge commits**, outside the predicate by construction; and (d) **single-parent commit CREATION by an ordinary one-step command** — plain `git cherry-pick <sha>`, plain `git revert <sha>`, `git am`, and a sequencer `--continue` of either. (d) is listed separately from (c) because the exclusion list previously named only "sequencer/rebase/am paths" and so read as if the ordinary one-step spellings were covered; they are not, and they produce exactly the unreviewed commit item 0 names as the live defect. Bucket (d) to **#783**. Those four are the item-12 cases this branch owes, or must name as follow-ups.<br>— **`fix/issue-780-zero-old-oid` (36 ahead)** and **`fix/issue-781-protected-ref-create` (36 ahead): before merge, each branch must include the item-12 minimum cases for the invariant IT claims to close — CONDITIONAL on *Still needs Chris* #2, per the graph.** Two corrections here, 2026-09-08, and neither decides anything. **(i) The enumeration is not supplied and must not be inferred from #622's.** #622's list is the merge-commit predicate's coverage and its exclusions; #780 closes a zero-old-oid force-update invariant and #781 a protected-ref-creation invariant — **different invariants, so the historical handover's command-shape list is #622's matrix, not theirs**. A worker on either branch cannot read "hostile committed env, git aliases, `GIT_*` redirection, `pull --ff-only`, sequencer `--continue`" off the #622 row and know what they owe. Supplying their enumerations would **re-plan sibling branches this plan is explicitly told not to re-plan** (see *Worker ownership*), so it is out of scope for a plan-only revision and is named as owed rather than guessed. **(ii) The "before merge" sentence is CONDITIONAL, not unconditional.** #780 and #781 are post-0827 **descendants** (the New-issues table buckets them under Ref-gate / #622 spawn), and the graph's `descendants ──► 12` edge was conditional on Chris #2 while the `0 remaining approved` edge names only #622 and #570. An earlier revision wrote these two sentences unconditionally, which contradicted the graph — and by this document's own precedence rule the graph wins. **This is reconciliation to the authoritative surface, not a loosening decided here.** **THE CONDITION HAS NOW RESOLVED, and the outcome is stated here rather than left for a reader to re-derive from the edge (2026-09-09, round 10, finding [14]).** Chris #2(ii) answered **the original seven only**, so `descendants ──► 12` **does not fire**: #780 and #781 are **not** item-0 work, they carry **no item-12 pre-merge obligation on this plan's authority**, and their matrix obligations follow the **item-12 SUCCESSOR route** instead. The word "conditional" above is retained as the record of why the sentences were written that way; **it is no longer a live condition, and no worker should read it as one still pending.**<br>*(Both previously read "grandfathered; follow-up issue to be numbered when opened". That satisfied this row's own `#NNN` requirement with a **promise** — no durable scope, no accountable handoff, and nothing scheduling the replacement, since descendant work is itself conditional on Chris #2. A rule waivable by writing a sentence is not a rule. Opening the two issues is outside this plan-only revision, so the disposition is set to the same enforceable sentence used for #622 rather than left as an unnumbered IOU; if Chris prefers genuine grandfathering, open the issues and paste the numbers here.)* | minimum required with fixes; broader expansion exploratory | **TODO** | Exercise entry point, evidence handoff and authorization outcome together, plus a valid recovery path. ## Worker ownership — do not re-plan these

A worktree existing is **not** evidence of work. **Provenance for the numeric columns, stated
exactly because this document's own argument is that unstamped numbers drift:** measured
`2026-09-08T12:00Z` with `git rev-list --count origin/main..<branch>` against
`origin/main = 727d465215dc2e2370b595fb1300746447461456`. A re-run on a later day will differ; treat
a mismatch as drift, not as an error in this table. (An earlier draft of this table recorded
`fix/issue-780-zero-old-oid` at 34 while the same day's measurement returned **36** — the column had
gone stale on the day it claimed to record.)

**Read the limit of that evidence honestly:** commits-ahead and last-commit-date establish branch
*activity*, not that a worker is assigned, still running, or intends to finish. A stale-but-nonzero
branch (639r, idle 15 d) and an abandoned one look identical here. Confirm live assignment with
Hermes/Herdr before treating a row as owned; nothing in this repository records that.

> **`ahead=0` does NOT mean nothing was done — an earlier draft of this section said it did, and
> that was wrong in a way that could have destroyed work.** Commits-ahead is blind to the index and
> the working tree. Re-measured 2026-09-08 with `git status --porcelain`: `busdriver-issue-789`
> carries **staged** edits to `scripts/lib/resolve-cli.sh`, `skills/litmus/scripts/run-review-loop.sh`
> and `tests/test-opencode-review-arm.sh`, an unstaged edit to `tests/test-agy-read-lane.sh`, plus
> untracked `tests/test-trusted-review-cli.sh` and a session handoff; `busdriver-issue-570` carries
> an untracked `docs/plans/2026-08-29-contained-review-entry-570.md`. Both were listed as empty.
> **Never dispose of a worktree on commits-ahead alone** — read all three signals below.

**Four** independent signals, never collapsed: **branch activity** (ahead / last commit),
**uncommitted work** (`git status --porcelain`), **base staleness and supersession** (what release
the branch sits on, and whether `origin/main` already carries a superset), and **confirmed
assignment** — which no signal in this repository can establish, so it stays *unknown* for every row
until Hermes/Herdr says otherwise.

**State the rule symmetrically, because the corrected version of this section installed the mirror
image of the error it fixed.** `commits-ahead = 0` never authorizes disposal — that was the original
defect. But `uncommitted-work-present` likewise never authorizes *scheduling*: preserving work and
scheduling it are different acts, and the second one requires diffing the content against
`origin/main` first. Preservation is unconditional; scheduling is not.

| Branch / worktree | Ahead | Last commit | Base / supersession vs `origin/main` | Uncommitted work + status (assignment: unknown for all) |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| `fix/issue-780-zero-old-oid` | 36 | 2026-09-07 | current-ish | **ACTIVE** — item 0 ref-gate |
| `fix/issue-802-glob-expansion-timeout` | 8 | 2026-09-07 | current-ish | **ACTIVE** — classifier |
| `fix/issue-781-protected-ref-create` | 36 | 2026-08-31 | current-ish | active, idle 7 d |
| `fix/issue-776-marker-case` | 28 | 2026-08-31 | current-ish | active — PR #800 open, DIRTY |
| `fix/issue-576-litmus-marker-hash` | 34 | 2026-08-30 | current-ish | #576 is **CLOSED**; residuals live in #793 #816 #835 — **待核實** whether this branch is superseded |
| `fix/issue-639-heredoc-prose` | 15 | 2026-08-23 | current-ish | PR #731 open, DIRTY, idle 15 d |
| `fix/grok-fail-salvage-coverage` | 1 | 2026-08-29 | current-ish | minimal |

No supersession checked | **uncommitted work present** — untracked `docs/plans/2026-08-29-contained-review-entry-570.md`. No commits, but a design doc exists; **preserve it**. Preservation is not scheduling |
| `fix/issue-789-trusted-review-cli` | **0** | — | HEAD `2faaef15` = **2.1.4**, ~11 releases behind. **SUPERSEDED ON MAIN by `bdb9b776` (#803/#810)** — verify residue only | **uncommitted work present** — 3 staged files, 1 unstaged, 1 untracked new test, plus a session handoff. **Measured 2026-09-08:** its untracked `tests/test-trusted-review-cli.sh` is **642 lines**; `git show 727d4652:tests/test-trusted-review-cli.sh` is **1,786 lines**. The worktree content is an earlier **precursor**, not an identical copy and not unique work. `bdb9b776` also rewrote `scripts/lib/resolve-cli.sh` and touched `skills/litmus/scripts/run-review-loop.sh` and `tests/test-opencode-review-arm.sh` — the same four paths this worktree holds. **Preserve; do not schedule without diffing for unique residue** |

**Assignment is unknown for every row, including these.** `fix/issue-622-merge-commit-gate`,
`fix/issue-780-zero-old-oid`, `fix/issue-781-protected-ref-create`, `fix/issue-776-marker-case` and
`fix/issue-639-heredoc-prose` show **recent branch activity; assignment unverified** — that is not
the same as having an owner, and this section has already ruled the inference out. `#570` and `#789`
show **zero commits and uncommitted work present**; they are **unowned, not empty**.

**Hermes/Herdr confirmation is a precondition of both scheduling and retirement**, for every row.
Stated once here; *Progress since the original plan* and *Recommended next actions* defer to this
sentence rather than restating it.

## Dependencies, order and acceptance for the remaining work

**This graph is the single ordered source of truth.** Where the status column, *Why this order*, or
*Recommended next actions* appear to disagree with it, the graph wins and the other surface is a
defect to fix — three review rounds found exactly that class of drift, so do not reconcile them by
reading intent.

                             milestone the left side brings about. A NUMBERED ITEM, or a
                             conjunction of numbered items, MUST stand on its left: a milestone
                             never produces another milestone.
                             `══▶` IS NECESSARY, NOT SUFFICIENT — CORRECTED 2026-09-09
                             (round 12). It says the left side produces the ARTIFACT the
                             milestone is granted over; it does NOT say that reaching the left
                             side reaches the milestone. Where a milestone additionally requires
                             an operator ACCEPTANCE or any conjunct beyond the artifact, that
                             conjunct is stated in the milestone's own node and is equally
                             binding. The earlier wording gave this glyph sufficiency semantics,
                             so `2i AND 2p ══▶ "protocol-approved"` read as making the milestone
                             reachable without Chris's acceptance — which its own node
                             immediately denies ("neither one nor both together reach this node
                             without the acceptance"), leaving the graph's supreme surface
                             contradicting the node it points at, on the milestone that gates
                             every mutating half.
                           MILESTONE-TO-MILESTONE DEPENDENCY uses `──►` under the existing
                             right-side rule — `"X" ──► "Y"` says Y is a PREREQUISITE of X, never
                             something X produces. CORRECTED 2026-09-09 (round 11): `══▶`
                             previously claimed to be the ONLY glyph permitted with a milestone on
                             both sides, which left no way to write a prerequisite between two
                             milestones and forced one to be written as production — whereupon
                             this graph's own supremacy rule beat the node text defining the
                             milestone.
                         The single glyph was genuinely ambiguous, not merely terse: read
                         under the dependency rule, `2i AND 2p ──► "protocol-approved" ──►
                         "baseline captured"` said 2i and 2p were BLOCKED ON
                         protocol-approved and protocol-approved BLOCKED ON baseline
                         captured — the exact inversion that re-created the cycle round 7
                         removed. Every milestone-producing edge now carries `══▶`.
                         ADDED 2026-09-08 (round 8, M25). Item 3's acceptance is stated once
                         in item 3's cell and not restated here; 7b registers a NEW PreToolUse
                         hook with Bash coverage, so without this edge each contaminates the
                         other's before/after cohort. The one alternative — measuring 7b's
                         cost inside item 3's after-cohort — is acceptable only if item 3's
                         acceptance says so BEFORE collection starts, never retroactively.
                         A third conjunct; 7b's other gates are on the 7b edge, not restated.
#622 ──► Chris #9        waive the blueprint-review record, or run blueprint-review on ADR 0051
